# Tasks: Phase 3 — Live sessions

**Input**: Design documents from `/specs/004-sessions/`

**Prerequisites**: [plan.md](./plan.md), [spec.md](./spec.md), [research.md](./research.md), [data-model.md](./data-model.md), [contracts/](./contracts/), [quickstart.md](./quickstart.md)

**Tests**: Included, and not optional, as in Phases 1 and 2: tests before implementation, each observed red for the right reason; every job with its double-run spec (R-API-14), the four new ones included; every procedure under the coverage floor, `modules/chat` joining it (T054); every procedure that issues a token or reads a session, its chat or its counterparty in the adversarial suite, both directions (FR-496); every video event in a spec produced by the stand-in harness over HTTP (T010, contracts/video.md), as the gateway's harness is for payments (`003:T013`); every lock and FK-insert path checked against `003:T005`'s two rules, with a probe in `tests/db-integrity/` where the database cannot hold the rule (data-model.md §5). Every run is on `ijazah_integrity` only (both URLs checked, `IJAZAH_DB_SPECS=1`), never on `ijazah`.

**Organization**: The carried tasks first (they keep their lines, notes and `[x]` marks in their own files; this file places them, and writes three of them as its own tasks, as `003:T010` wrote `002:T143`), then the foundations, the seven stories of spec.md in priority order, the proofs and the close, and Phase 4's Spec Kit cycle. Task ids follow the order of the batches, B33 to B43, so a story's tasks are not contiguous; T058, added on the analysis (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05), is in B42, T059, added on the third analysis (a ruling's revision, the owner's decision of 2026-11-05), is in B39; T060 (the complaint's API) and T061 (the restore's API), added on the decision of no automatic termination, are in B40, and T062 (the signed-in rate limits), added on the decisions of the administrator's control and the rate limits, is in B36 (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05). The task pass of the decisions answering the administrator's control inventory and of the live channel (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05; research R-323 to R-327) adds four: T063, the live channel's server side, in B36; T064, the manual compensation case, in B39; T065 and T066, the live channel on the phone and its remaining producers, in a new batch B40a; the live channel's three tasks have their own section, "The live channel", before the proofs, and R-323's answered gaps extend existing tasks (T001, T004, T008, T014, T024, T027, T033, T047, T048, T050, T055, T059). B37's tasks commit (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-09 and of 2026-11-10 on B37's conflict check) adds two: T067, the room ended at the session's scheduled end (FR-501), in B40a; T068, the keep-alive service's stop and the microphone check (PR #130's Minor 5), in the logo build of 12/1 to 12/5, B44, outside the phase's days (PLAN.md, «The road to the store»). The batches at the end are the unit of delivery: one branch, one pull request, one review by Saif's review session, which checks every lock and FK-insert path against `003:T005`'s rules, at most six tasks (PLAN.md; plan.md, "Batch plan").

**The owner's window rule of 2026-11-05** (plan.md, "Decisions recorded", decision 9's H1 to H5 and decision 14; research R-320): no time is shown or offered that starts before the window it waits on can end. The tasks that apply it each name the window and what is hidden, with a spec for each bound: T021 (a cancellation request), T027 (reservations, H3, and the confirmation's new hold for a released one), T028 (the system's proposal, H1), T029 (a make-up's booking reads the reservations again), T031 (the counter-proposal's options, H2 and H4), T032 (the release of a reserved time, H3, and the choice window's end, H5), T005 and T028 (a remedy frozen by a student's suspension, and offered again with a new full choice window after the reinstatement). The owner's decisions on the analysis (plan.md, "Decisions on the analysis") add the first-session lead and fix the windows at the offer: with A the remedy's `snapshot_answer_window_hours` (72), L its `snapshot_first_session_lead_hours` (24) and C its `choice_closes_at`, **H1** the proposal starts no earlier than C + A + L and overlaps no open hold of either party; **H2** the counter options start no earlier than max(now + A + L, C). The decisions on the third analysis (plan.md, "Decisions on the third analysis", items 2 and 7) add two: T028's offer itself waits for the session's dispute window (`dispute_window_hours`, 72 h), any dispute raised in it and any revision (T059), so no remedy, proposal or counter option exists before the latest of the window's end, the last resolution and the last revision; and T032's retry of a refused proposal books nothing starting within L of the attempt, nor over an open hold or a reservation of either party. The decision of no automatic termination (research R-320's two rows of 2026-11-05) adds T061: a restore's recreated sessions start no earlier than the restore plus L and overlap no session, open hold or reservation of either party, and each remedy it offers again takes a new full choice window, its proposal obeying H1 after the recreated sessions; the complaint (T060) offers no time.

## Format: `[ID] [P?] [Story] Description`

- **[P]**: Can run in parallel — different files, no dependency on incomplete work
- **[Story]**: US1–US7, mapping to spec.md
- A task of `specs/003-booking/tasks.md` is written `003:T059`, one of `specs/002-identity/tasks.md` `002:T144`; a bare id is this file's. Every task names its file paths.

## Path Conventions

The three applications of Phase 0 (plan.md, "Source Code"): `apps/mobile/src/` (`app/` routes, `features/`, `design/`, `lib/`, `i18n/`), `apps/admin/src/` (`pages/`), `apps/api/src/` (`modules/<module>/`, `platform/`, `surfaces/`, `jobs/`, `main.ts`, `worker.ts`), `packages/`, `prisma/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`. Every batch is cut from `develop` as `Ejazah_app/004-sessions/<ids>` and its commits say `Ejazah_app(004-sessions)` (plan.md, header). A task's note goes under its line, as in Phases 0 to 2.

  > **Follow-up (B33, T001, 2026-11-06): the schema gate refuses the counter-proposal's local time beside `counter_timezone`.** `tests/db-integrity/schema-check.spec.ts` («the migrated schema passes the check (T070) › reports no violation, read as the application role», and the violation case's count) fails with `local-time session_events.counter_start_time_local` and `local-time session_remedies.counter_start_time_local`. Cause: `scripts/schema-check.ts`' `local-time` rule accepts a `_local` column only in a table holding a column named exactly `timezone`, while data-model.md §2.3 and §2.4 and ERD §5 put the zone beside these columns in `counter_timezone` (data-model.md §2.3: «R-TIME-3's gate asks a zone beside a `_local` column»). Not fixed in T001: the task names only `DECLARED_CACHES` in that script, and how the gate should read a zone column of another name (for instance `<prefix>_timezone` beside `<prefix>_…_local`) is the owner's to decide (constitution R-TIME-3, R-TIME-5). **Resolved** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-06, inside T001): the gate accepts a `_local` column beside a `timezone` column, or beside `<p>_timezone` when the `_local` column's name starts with `<p>_`, and refuses one with neither or whose only zone has another prefix; `tests/db-integrity/schema-check.spec.ts` holds the three cases and the two violations are gone; constitution 2.6.2 (PATCH) words R-TIME-3's and R-TIME-5's enforcement so, and the ERD's, ARCHITECTURE's and PLAN.md's headers name 2.6.2.

`transaction.repository.ts` gains `runRetryingDeadlockOnce(transactions, work, refusal, trace)`, running `work` through `Transactions.run` as `transactionsOver` runs every transaction, once more in a new transaction when the first ends in a deadlock, `refusal()` thrown on a second deadlock, any other failure rethrown unchanged, never a third attempt; every deadlock logged (the owner's decision of 2026-11-06 on the small check after the sixth analysis; contracts/kernel.md): `trace` = `{ log, operation, ids }`, `operation` the calling procedure's name given by the caller, `ids` closed to `{ remedyId }` or `{ enrollmentId }`, one `warn` line `{ operation, attempt, ...ids }` per deadlock (`attempt` 1 or 2) and one more `{ operation, attempts: 2, ...ids }` when the refusal is thrown, no other field, nothing personal, never the error's text or the statement; with a spec in a new `apps/api/src/platform/db/transaction.repository.spec.ts` (one deadlock then success: two runs, the result, one `warn` line; two deadlocks: two runs, the refusal, three `warn` lines;   > **Follow-up (B33, the full `pnpm test` gate on `ijazah_integrity`, 2026-11-07): a lock-wait probe orders two client-side callbacks the database does not order.** `tests/db-integrity/session-locks.spec.ts:190` («lockRows on Phase 3’s tables › disputes: a second lock of the same row waits until the first ends») logged `second took` before `first ended` once, under the full run's load. The wait itself was proved (`seenWaiting` saw the second backend waiting on the lock); the cause is the assertion in `waitsOn` (line 117): `first ended` is pushed when the first transaction's commit has returned to the client, while the database frees the lock at the commit itself, so the second transaction can take the row and push first. Not a lock defect. The run: the scratchpad's `b33/gate-05-test.txt`. Fix: `waitsOn` logs `first released` when it calls `release()`, before the commit, which the second cannot pass, and asserts that order.

(Minor 4, **B37, in T025's lane before T026**, so the refusals T021, T023 and T026 add are logged once from their first line) A refused `sessions.get`, `sessions.join` or outage record is logged twice, once by the module (`apps/api/src/modules/sessions/sessions.service.ts:148`) and once by `onError` (`apps/api/src/app.module.ts:378`): the module's refusal carries the mark «logged» as the input guard's does (`isRefusalLoggedByKind`, `apps/api/src/surfaces/procedures.ts:502`), and `onError` writes no second line for it; no third exception is added to the note above (Q6 a).

**Checkpoint**: the schema, the locks, the intervals, the port and the harness exist; the stories can start; the signed-in rate limits (T062) land in B36, before the first administrator procedure that declares one.

---

## Phase 2: User Story 1 — A teacher and a student meet in a live session (Priority: P1)

**Goal**: Inside the early-join lead, each participant opens a pre-session screen, receives a token minted by the server for that session and that person, and meets the other in a one-to-one room.

**Independent Test**: With the stand-in, open a session inside and outside the join period as each participant, as another enrollment's participants and as an opposite-gender account: only the two participants receive a token, inside the period only; on two Android phones over mobile data, hold a session with the provider, switching networks in the middle (spec.md, US1; quickstart.md §2 to §4).

## Phase 3: User Story 2 — The session's outcome is settled from evidence (Priority: P1)

**Goal**: The provider's signed webhooks record each connection; a sweep after the scheduled end plus the delay settles each session by SRS §3.7.5's table; an enrollment's Teacher-No-Shows are counted and the enrollment marked for administrators at the configured count, no count ending it (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05); an enrollment completes.

**Independent Test**: Through the harness, deliver signed events for each row of the table, a disconnect and reconnection, a duplicate, events out of order, a lost final leave and a bad signature; run the job, run it again; check each outcome, the count, the mark at the configured count, and that no count ends an enrollment (spec.md, US2; quickstart.md §1 and §2).

The latest events: a `HashAggregate` on `(session_id, event_type)`; at this table's size the planner scans its 527 rows (0.2 ms), and the `session_id` index serves it as the table grows. No index added (R-DB-9). **The run's duration with the stand-in** (warm runs of the whole job over the spec file's own sessions on `ijazah_integrity`, rounds 2 and 3 of 3): no session due a close, 20.1 and 27.9 ms; one, 21.8 and 25.1 ms; twenty settled sessions due a close, 27.5 and 25.1 ms (round 1, cold, 156.2 ms with none). The probe's second attempt placed `scheduled` sessions, which the run also settled, on `ijazah_integrity` only (the test database); it was discarded and run again with settled sessions. With LiveKit each call adds its round trip, up to the adapter's 10 s timeout, in sequence. Specs, each seen red first, the five «never called» cases by two mutants of the read: `evaluation.service.spec.ts` (`endScheduledRooms`, :485, with the retry period of 0 refused), `apps/api/src/jobs/evaluate-sessions.spec.ts` (the order), and `tests/sessions/evaluate-sessions.spec.ts` (T067's describe, :775, and the double-run spec with a session due a close).
  > **Follow-up under T067 (PR #135's review, round 1, follow-up 2, 2026-11-12; the owner's decision (a) of the same day): Phase 6, overlapping runs.** BullMQ 6.3.11 schedules the next run of `evaluate-sessions` as one starts, and the worker delivers up to `QUEUE_CONCURRENCY` jobs at once (default 4, `apps/api/src/platform/config/env.schema.ts:166`; `apps/api/src/jobs/queue.ts:159`). With a slow LiveKit, a run still inside `endScheduledRooms` (`apps/api/src/jobs/evaluate-sessions.ts:98`) can overlap the next, so one session's room may be asked to end, and a failure reported, twice in a minute, not «at most one call per minute» as PR #135's body first said (corrected). Harmless: `DeleteRoom` is idempotent and the adapter takes `not_found` as success. Capping the step's duration, or a run lock, is weighed in Phase 6 with the queue's other jobs. **Per run (PR #135's review, round 2, follow-up, 2026-11-12):** T067's line («at most one call per minute of the bound per session») and the note under it («at most one per session per minute of the bound», «at most one per due session per minute») hold per run, overlapping runs aside.

**Checkpoint**: every outcome of SRS §3.7.5's table, the repeated-absence count and mark (no count ending an enrollment) and completion pass in CI from signed events alone.

## Phase 4: User Story 3 — Either party asks to cancel, and only agreement makes it free (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: A request with a reason, answered by the other party before the start; an administrator's cancellation with a reason; the requests counted on a user's record.

**Independent Test**: Request, accept, decline and let expire from each side; cancel as an administrator; check each status, the make-up after an acceptance, the case after the administrator's cancellation, and the counts (spec.md, US3; quickstart.md §5 and §6).

## Phase 5: User Story 4 — A session not consumed is made up, or refunded by a case (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: Once an outcome is final, one remedy per session: a proposal that never starts before the matter could still be decided, the student's choice and counter-proposals, the teacher's answer, the booking, or a case with no amount.

**Independent Test**: Produce each unconsumed outcome on both tracks; choose, accept, counter-propose (accepted and declined, then again), accept while a counter is pending, and let each window pass; check the make-up's number, its link, the enrollment's new end and slot release, the reserved times hidden from others, and the refund case's lack of any amount (spec.md, US4; quickstart.md §5).

- [x] T027 [US4] Reservations (FR-466; research R-311, "Reservation"; R-320; plan.md decision 2): `reservedTimesOf(tx, userIds, window)` in `apps/api/src/modules/sessions/`, exported by `index.ts`; booking's one overlap check (`apps/api/src/modules/booking/session-overlap.service.ts`) reads it for `booking.slots` and `checkout.hold`, each already holding both accounts' locks first (`003:T005` rule 2), so a reservation and a hold of the same people serialise; a `frozen` remedy reserves nothing. Phase 2's confirmation (`apps/api/src/modules/booking/confirmation.service.ts`) relies on the database's exclusions, which see no reservation, so its branch that places a new hold for a released one (FR-PAYG-19's late payment) reads `reservedTimesOf` too, under the accounts' locks it already holds, and takes its refusal branch (`slot_unavailable`, a compensation case) when a generated session would overlap a reservation of either party; its branch that converts a still-active hold needs nothing, no reservation being made over an open hold (F5, H2) (research R-311, "Reservation"; the owner's decision of 2026-11-05 on the analysis). **Window rule (H3)** — window: the system's proposal from its offer until the remedy settles (booked, or handed to a case); a pending counter-proposal during its answer window, until its decline, its withdrawal or the window's end; hidden: those occurrences, from `booking.slots`, refused by `checkout.hold` and refused to the confirmation's new hold for a released one, for the same teacher or the same student; specs per case, a spec that a late payment overlapping a reservation takes the refusal branch, and a probe in `tests/db-integrity/` racing a hold against a proposal for the same teacher and student (plan.md, "Post-Design Constitution Re-Check", point 4). ## Phase 6: User Story 5 — An administrator settles what the evidence could not (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: Disputes raised and frozen; the review queue, the disputes queue and the compensation-case queue resolved with a reason, audit-logged, moving no money.

**Independent Test**: Raise a dispute inside and outside the window; resolve it each way; resolve an Under-Threshold and a Provider-Outage session; resolve and dismiss a case; check the final outcome, the count, the audit entries and that no amount exists (spec.md, US5; quickstart.md §6).

## Phase 7: User Story 6 — The account's end reaches its sessions (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: A teacher's or a student's end cancels what remains and opens the cases it owes, on the action's transaction, through `StandingService`; a deleted party is named by a stand-in.

**Independent Test**: Suspend, revoke and ban a teacher with sessions scheduled and one in progress; delete a teacher's and a student's account; suspend, reinstate and ban a student; check tokens refused, sessions cancelled, the enrollments' status and reason, and one case per affected enrollment, in the action's transaction (spec.md, US6; quickstart.md §6).

### The 14-day ban, a teacher's suspension that pauses, and a deleted teacher's account (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12; SRS DEC-66 and DEC-67; ADR 0015 as amended)

**Goal**: A ban and a revocation can be lifted at any time, and any end an administrator started can be restored at any time, the slot held for 14 days (DEC-69); an administrator can end an enrollment directly (DEC-68); a teacher's suspension pauses; a restore owes no session twice and never follows a refund; a deleted teacher once approved keeps what fraud prevention needs; no account deletes itself with an ongoing enrollment.

**Order** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-12, Q7 and Q-L): B42a (T069 → T070 ∥ T071 → T073 → T072), then B42b (T074, T075), then B42c (T076 → T077, T080 → T081, T082 → T083), all before B43. T078 goes with the privacy policy (PLAN.md, Phase 6), T079 to the design days. Every task: red first (a stub or a named mutant, restored and `cmp`-checked), one log line of ids and a reason per refusal and per automatic action, prisma run with `CHECKPOINT_DISABLE=1 PRISMA_HIDE_UPDATE_MESSAGE=1`.

## Phase 8: User Story 7 — The two participants chat and exchange documents (Priority: P3)

**Goal**: Text and documents between the two parties of an enrollment, never an image, read-only once it has ended, purged after its last dispute window plus the retention.

**Independent Test**: Send text, a PDF, a text file, an image, an image renamed with a document's extension and a document given an image's content; only the documents are accepted, served through expiring links; the purge runs at the right instant and not while a dispute is open (spec.md, US7; quickstart.md §7).

  > **Follow-up under T045 (B41, lane C's report, the review session, 2026-11-12; covered, PR #136's review, round 2, follow-up, recorded by B42's tasks commit): a message merged during a restart's read.** The open chat places a `chat` event into its first page by `messageId` (`apps/mobile/src/features/shared/chat/api.ts`, `placeMessage`, :64), and a (re)start of the live channel reads the first page again (`lib/live-channel.ts`, `LIVE_QUERY_ROOTS`); a message whose event lands just after that read was sent, and before its answer, can be overwritten by the answer, which was read before the message existed. The next (re)start, or reopening the chat, reads it back. **Covered; no code.** While that read is in flight, `placeMessage` takes its `fetching` branch (`apps/mobile/src/features/shared/chat/api.ts:68`) and invalidates the page (`:69`); `invalidateQueries` refetches through `refetchQueries`, whose `cancelRefetch: options.cancelRefetch ?? true` (`@tanstack/query-core` 5.104.4, `build/modern/queryClient.js:360-363`) cancels the read in flight and reads again; the event is published only after the commit (`apps/api/src/modules/chat/chat.service.ts:240`, `live.afterCommit`), so the new read includes the message.
  > **Follow-up under T045 (PR #136's review, round 1, follow-up 2, 2026-11-12; no code in B41): the chat's entry on an enrollment cancelled before its confirmation.** `chatReachable` (`apps/mobile/src/features/shared/chat/messages.ts`) is true for every status but `pending_payment`, so an enrollment cancelled while awaiting payment (`payment_expired`, `checkout_abandoned` …) shows the chat's entry (`ChatEntry` in `EnrollmentDetail.tsx`) to an empty, read-only chat, though FR-488 gives a chat only from the confirmation. Harmless: `chat.list` answers an empty page and every write is refused `CHAT_CLOSED`. The phone cannot tell the two `cancelled` cases apart without a field such as `confirmedAt`, so a contract change comes first.

**Checkpoint**: the corpus is refused, documents arrive through expiring links, the purge keeps what is open.

## The live channel (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05; research R-324)

**Goal**: While the app is open, one channel per signed-in device carries chat messages and the signals that make the open screens read again; the server addresses every event and sends it after its commit; nothing while the app is closed until Phase 4 (spec FR-427 to FR-429, FR-437, FR-438).

**Independent Test**: Open two streams for A and B and one for C; send a chat and request a cancellation between A and B: A and B receive them and C nothing; suspend A: A's streams end after their `account` event; stop the API with streams open: it stops without waiting (quickstart.md §1, §4).

  > **Follow-up under T066 (PR #135's review, round 1, follow-up 1, 2026-11-12; the owner's decision (a) of the same day): Phase 4, with the gender correction.** A `chat` event is added inside `chat.send`'s keyed effect (`tellParties`, `apps/api/src/modules/chat/chat.service.ts:232`, called at `:307`) after the genders were compared under the sender's lock, and sent when the handler resolves (`apps/api/src/surfaces/procedures.ts:434-439`). A gender correction committing in the few milliseconds between that commit and the flush could deliver one message written while the two were of the same gender. No correction exists today (SRS FR-GENDER-7 is Phase 4's), so the window cannot be reached; when FR-GENDER-7 is built, it decides whether the flush checks again or the window is accepted.

**Checkpoint**: the live channel carries chat and the signals while the app is open, the server addresses every event, and shutdown never waits on a stream.

- **US1** and **US2**: T011 after T007 and T009; T012 after T010; T014 after T013 and T015 (T014's sweep calls T015's completion check, O1); T015 after T008; T017 before T018 to T019 (the build on the phone first).

- **US4** after US3: T027 before T028 (the proposal avoids reservations), T029 and T030 before T031 and T032.

- **Proofs and the close** last; T057 the same day.

### Parallel within a batch (the owner's decision of 2026-10-26)

Independent tasks of one batch run as parallel lanes, the shared files (`main.ts`, `worker.ts`, `context.ts`, `admin.router.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/index.ts` and `admin/index.ts`, the snapshot, `ar.json` and `en.json`) integrated by the implementing session. For example, in B34: T007, T008 and T009 touch different files, while T005 and T006 share booking's subscriber and run in that order; in B42 the five panel tasks T047 to T051 are five lanes, and T058 follows all five, last in the batch, since it switches their pages' generic-error paths to the panel's `RATE_LIMITED` helper (the owner's decision of 2026-11-06 on the fifth analysis); in B40, T060 and T061 run in that order beside the chat lanes; in B39, T064 follows T035; in B40a, T065 and T066 are two lanes sharing T063's publisher, T065's webhook publish and T066's producers touching different files, and T067 is a third, on the sweep's files alone; in B37, three lanes, T021 then T022, T023 then T024 (one router and one contract), T025 then T026 (the owner's decision of 2026-11-10 on B37's conflict check, Q8).

(2) **the payment confirmation made live**, after (1): `confirm-enrollment` runs in the worker, and Phase 2's screens poll it every `checkout_confirming_poll_seconds` until then; (3) **for T057's Phase 4 cycle**: the cancellation request is the first thing that needs push, since it expires at the session's start and a counterparty whose app is closed would never learn of it in time; a forward-only migration drops it, and `prisma/schema/sessions.prisma` (`providerRoomId`) and ERD §5 lose it (ERD changelog row 33 notes it unused until then; `createRoom`'s comment naming it is fixed in T009); (6) **a reset script for `ijazah_integrity`** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-07 on B33): it drops and creates the database `ijazah_integrity` by that exact name and migrates it, refusing unless `current_database()` on the connection it checks names `ijazah_integrity`, and it runs only with the owner's approval each time, never from a spec or a CI step; why: committed probe rows pile up there (25,610 `verification_documents` rows held a key on 2026-11-06), which made `003:T080`'s cases slow, and rows written before a rule lands can block its migration, as rule 46 refused 74 closed cases in B33 (deleted by the owner's decision, their full rows kept in the scratchpad); until then nothing there is deleted; counted in no batch. (7) **`heldFileOwnersAmong` bounded** (2026-11-07, PR #127's review, round 1, Minor 2): its read, `enrollmentPartiesAmong` (`apps/api/src/modules/booking/held-files.repository.ts:14`), reads every enrollment of each owner asked, in any status, with no bound, and `held-files.service.ts` then sends all of their ids in one `IN` list over `disputes` (moderation's `enrollmentsWithOpenMatterAmong`); fine at MVP scale (an erasure page's owners, a deletion's one), reviewed before launch; counted in no batch. (8) **Where LiveKit serves the video, before launch** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-07, B34): T009's check on the free project (EU, Frankfurt) was served by a node of the region `ojerusalem1a` (Israel), LiveKit Cloud choosing the nearest edge itself; two questions before launch: on which plan region pinning, or the region group «me» named in `docs/adr/0003-video-baseline.md` (its References, line 133), is available; and moving video data, children's among it, out of Egypt under the PDPL, added to counsel's questions on hosting; ADR 0003 is not changed now; must be settled before launch. (9) **A session whose evaluation fails every minute** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-08, B35, T014; Phase 6): `evaluate-sessions` catches a session's failure, rolls back that session's transaction alone, writes one error line `{ sessionId }` and reports the error to error tracking (`apps/api/src/modules/sessions/evaluation.service.ts:267`), and the next run, a minute later, tries it again; a session that fails every time sends one report a minute and can use up the error tracking's quota; bounded before launch; counted in no batch. (10) **`evaluate-sessions`' reads with no index** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-08, B35, T014; Phase 6): the cancelled sessions inside their join period (`cancelledInJoinPeriod`, `apps/api/src/modules/sessions/evaluation.repository.ts:135`) are read by status and times with no index, and the due read (`dueForEvaluation`, line 48) finds its `disputed` sessions outside `sessions_scheduled_end_at_scheduled_idx`, which holds `status = 'scheduled'` alone (`prisma/migrations/20261007000300_scheduled_sessions_index/migration.sql:3`); each is a scan of `sessions` once a minute, fine at MVP scale; measured before launch and an index added if needed; counted in no batch. A follow-up a batch finds is written in this file with its cause and stays here until the owner times it. `specs/003-booking/tasks.md`'s Not-scheduled row stays there (research R-319); T099 and T093 of that file touch screens this phase edits and are listed for the owner, not timed (11) **CI's coverage command runs the database specs twice** (found by B35, PR #129, 2026-11-08; the owner's decision: recorded only, CI unchanged): `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, stage 2, line 138, `pnpm test -- --coverage --exclude 'tests/db-integrity/**'`: `--exclude` on the command line does not take out vitest's `db-integrity` project, so stage 2 collects the 57 database files as well (381 files with and without it, `vitest list`), and stage 4 (`pnpm test:db-integrity`, line 305) runs them again. Changing stage 2 moves the repositories out of the coverage, and CI may turn red as on PR #127, so it waits for a decision of its own. (12) **The APK's permissions reviewed before the store** (Phase 6; found by B36's build, the owner's decision of 2026-11-08; the note under T017): the storage permissions on Android 12 and earlier (`READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, `maxSdkVersion=32`), whether the file uploads need them, and `blockedPermissions` in `apps/mobile/app.json` for those not needed (a new build). (13) **«Enrollment completed» on the outage record's path, written after the commit** (B36, T063's follow-up, PR #129's review, round 1, Minor 3; the owner's decision of 2026-11-08: before launch, nothing built in B36): `sessions.outages.record` completes an enrollment inside the record's transaction (`apps/api/src/modules/sessions/outages.service.ts:244`, `completion.completeIfDue`), and the line is written right after it answers true (`:245`, on the record's transaction; lane C moved it out of `completeIfDue`, and the sweep writes its own after its commit), so a failed audit write or commit leaves a line for a completion that never happened; why it waits: the administrator kind's flush point (`ctx.live.flush()`, `apps/api/src/surfaces/procedures.ts:706`, once `transactions.run` at `:676-677` has committed) carries live events only, so a log line after the commit needs a new member on `RequestLive` (`apps/api/src/platform/live/live-publisher.ts`), for example `afterCommitDo(action)`, drained at that same flush point and reached from the record through `publisherOn(tx)`; until then the line stays inside the transaction; the line stays one, `{ enrollmentId }`, and none when completion is postponed; (14) **the video webhook's helpers copied from the payment webhook** (the review at 3a14fc7, follow-up (c) under T019; placed by the owner's decision of 2026-11-10 on B37's conflict check, Q6): `declaredLengthOf`, `readRawBody`, `send` and `refuse` in `apps/api/src/modules/sessions/video-webhook.controller.ts` are the same as in `apps/api/src/modules/payments/webhook.controller.ts`, with `BodyAbortedError` and `tooLarge` beside them, later one helper beside `apps/api/src/platform/guards/webhook-signature.guard.ts`; why it waits: no behaviour changes, so it is a refactor for the full-codebase review after the MVP (PLAN.md, Phase 6), not a batch of this phase. |

## Implementation Strategy

The MVP of this phase is US1 and US2: a session is joined only by its two participants and settles from the provider's signed events alone, after every account's end is in place (US6's first tasks, B33 and B34). US3, US4 and US5 complete what an outcome owes and what an administrator decides; US7 is the descope ladder's rung (in-session file sharing goes before text chat, PLAN.md). Each batch merges to `develop` before the next starts; a batch that grows past six tasks or its day is stated when it happens, not forecast.

**Two tracks at once (the owner's plan of 2026-11-15; PLAN.md, «How a phase is run» as amended on 2026-11-16).** From 11/16 to 11/19 two batches may be in flight at once, one per track, each with its own implementer pair: track 1 Phase 4 (`specs/005-certification/`), on `ijazah_integrity`; track 2 the money (`specs/003-booking/tasks.md` T101, T102, T104, T105, and ADR 0024's programme), on `ijazah_integrity_b`. They never write the same file; their migrations merge in timestamp order and are proved together on one database at the integration. This file's rule above holds inside each track.
